Maybe a little uncomfortable, like something feels unfinished or not lined up right. But I can stop sometimes, especially if someone’s watching or tells me directly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like putting things into patterns or doing certain rituals when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, mostly it’s at the warehouse because that’s where the boxes and the forklift and all that stuff are. I guess that’s what people notice, like I’ll kind of freeze up with my arms raised near the forklift, or copy the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and sometimes I move the boxes into little patterns even when people are telling me to stop.\n\nAt home it’s not really like that, I don’t think. It’s more tied to work. I know it sounds weird when I say it out loud, but I’m not trying to cause problems. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these patterns and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like something separate from yourself?\n\nPatient: I guess… it feels like me when I’m doing it, if that makes sense. Not like some totally separate thing taking over. It’s just… I get stuck in it at the warehouse, like with the forklift I’ll freeze with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, and the boxes start feeling like they should go in these little patterns.\n\nI know everyone’s yelling for me to stop sometimes, and I’m not trying to be difficult. It just sort of happens before I can explain it. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you want to do, or something that you feel like you shouldn't be doing?\n\nPatient: I don’t really want to be doing it if it’s causing trouble… I mean, I don’t go in thinking, “I’m going to mess things up today.” It just kind of happens at the warehouse.\n\nLike with the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into these little patterns, and then people are yelling for me to stop. I know I probably shouldn’t be doing it then. I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult… it just feels hard to explain in the moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really outside of work. Like, I don’t catch myself checking the door over and over or repeating stuff at home, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMaybe I double-check normal things sometimes, but I don’t think it’s a problem. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you wish you could stop or control better?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do wish I could control it better. I don’t like that it makes everyone upset or slows things down.\n\nI mean, I know I’ve said it already, but at the warehouse I’ll just kind of freeze by the forklift with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start putting boxes into these weird little patterns, and even when people are yelling at me to stop, I don’t always stop right away. I’m not trying to be a problem. I just… I wish I could catch it before it happens, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that are a part of you or something that you feel are foreign to yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… it feels like me in the sense that I’m the one doing it, so I don’t want to act like it’s someone else. But it doesn’t really feel like something I’d choose, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into those little patterns… it’s embarrassing, honestly. Especially when people are yelling for me to stop and I still don’t stop right away. That doesn’t feel like how I want to be. It feels kind of… not foreign exactly, but not like the normal me either. Is that an okay way to say it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to be doing, or something that you wish you could stop doing because they don't fit with how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to be doing it, no. I mean… I guess part of me tries to tell myself it’s not that bad, but it doesn’t really fit with how I see myself.\n\nLike at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals, or moving boxes into weird little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop… that’s not something I’m proud of. I’m not doing it because I think it’s fun or because I want attention. I wish I could stop before it gets to that point.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I think I would like to stop. It does bother me, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it.

I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you want to do, or something that you feel like you shouldn't be doing?\n\nPatient: I don’t really want to be doing it if it’s causing trouble… I mean, I don’t go in thinking, “I’m going to mess things up today.” It just kind of happens at the warehouse.\n\nLike with the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into these little patterns, and then people are yelling for me to stop. I know I probably shouldn’t be doing it then. I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult… it just feels hard to explain in the moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really outside of work. Like, I don’t catch myself checking the door over and over or repeating stuff at home, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMaybe I double-check normal things sometimes, but I don’t think it’s a problem. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you wish you could stop or control better?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do wish I could control it better. I don’t like that it makes everyone upset or slows things down.\n\nI mean, I know I’ve said it already, but at the warehouse I’ll just kind of freeze by the forklift with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start putting boxes into these weird little patterns, and even when people are yelling at me to stop, I don’t always stop right away. I’m not trying to be a problem. I just… I wish I could catch it before it happens, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that are a part of you or something that you feel are foreign to yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… it feels like me in the sense that I’m the one doing it, so I don’t want to act like it’s someone else. But it doesn’t really feel like something I’d choose, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into those little patterns… it’s embarrassing, honestly. Especially when people are yelling for me to stop and I still don’t stop right away. That doesn’t feel like how I want to be. It feels kind of… not foreign exactly, but not like the normal me either. Is that an okay way to say it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to be doing, or something that you wish you could stop doing because they don't fit with how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to be doing it, no. I mean… I guess part of me tries to tell myself it’s not that bad, but it doesn’t really fit with how I see myself.\n\nLike at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals, or moving boxes into weird little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop… that’s not something I’m proud of. I’m not doing it because I think it’s fun or because I want attention. I wish I could stop before it gets to that point.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to stop doing because they bother you or make you feel uncomfortable with yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I would like to stop. It does bother me, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it.\n\nLike, at work, when I end up standing there by the forklift with my arms up, or copying the beeping noises and the hand signals, or putting boxes into these odd little patterns… and people are yelling at me to stop, and I still don’t right away. That makes me feel pretty uncomfortable with myself after. I don’t want to be that guy at the warehouse causing trouble.\n\nI’m not trying to make excuses. I just… yeah, I wish I could stop doing it. Does that answer it right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you want to do, or something that you feel like you shouldn't be doing?\n\nPatient: I don’t really want to be doing it if it’s causing trouble… I mean, I don’t go in thinking, “I’m going to mess things up today.” It just kind of happens at the warehouse.\n\nLike with the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into these little patterns, and then people are yelling for me to stop. I know I probably shouldn’t be doing it then. I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult… it just feels hard to explain in the moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really outside of work. Like, I don’t catch myself checking the door over and over or repeating stuff at home, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMaybe I double-check normal things sometimes, but I don’t think it’s a problem. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you wish you could stop or control better?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do wish I could control it better. I don’t like that it makes everyone upset or slows things down.\n\nI mean, I know I’ve said it already, but at the warehouse I’ll just kind of freeze by the forklift with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start putting boxes into these weird little patterns, and even when people are yelling at me to stop, I don’t always stop right away. I’m not trying to be a problem. I just… I wish I could catch it before it happens, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that are a part of you or something that you feel are foreign to yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… it feels like me in the sense that I’m the one doing it, so I don’t want to act like it’s someone else. Especially when people are yelling for me to stop and I still don’t stop right away. That doesn’t feel like how I want to be. It feels kind of… not foreign exactly, but not like the normal me either. Is that an okay way to say it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to be doing, or something that you wish you could stop doing because they don't fit with how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to be doing it, no. I’m not doing it because I think it’s fun or because I want attention. I wish I could stop before it gets to that point.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to stop doing because they bother you or make you feel uncomfortable with yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I would like to stop. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these patterns and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like something separate from yourself?\n\nPatient: I guess… it feels like me when I’m doing it, if that makes sense. Not like some totally separate thing taking over. It’s just… I get stuck in it at the warehouse, like with the forklift I’ll freeze with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, and the boxes start feeling like they should go in these little patterns.\n\nI know everyone’s yelling for me to stop sometimes, and I’m not trying to be difficult. It just sort of happens before I can explain it. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you want to do, or something that you feel like you shouldn't be doing?\n\nPatient: I don’t really want to be doing it if it’s causing trouble… I mean, I don’t go in thinking, “I’m going to mess things up today.” It just kind of happens at the warehouse.\n\nLike with the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into these little patterns, and then people are yelling for me to stop. I know I probably shouldn’t be doing it then. I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult… it just feels hard to explain in the moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really outside of work. I don’t like that it makes everyone upset or slows things down.\n\nI mean, I know I’ve said it already, but at the warehouse I’ll just kind of freeze by the forklift with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start putting boxes into these weird little patterns, and even when people are yelling at me to stop, I don’t always stop right away. I’m not trying to be a problem. I just… I wish I could catch it before it happens, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that are a part of you or something that you feel are foreign to yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… it feels like me in the sense that I’m the one doing it, so I don’t want to act like it’s someone else. It’s more like when I’m there, it feels right to put them that way, like the boxes sort of make more sense in little patterns. I know people get upset and tell me to stop, and I try to listen, but sometimes I just keep doing it before I really think about it. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uncomfortable when you can't put the boxes into those patterns?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. Maybe a little uncomfortable, like something feels unfinished or not lined up right. But I can stop sometimes, especially if someone’s watching or tells me directly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like putting things into patterns or doing certain rituals when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, mostly it’s at the warehouse because that’s where the boxes and the forklift and all that stuff are. I guess that’s what people notice, like I’ll kind of freeze up with my arms raised near the forklift, or copy the beeping sounds or the hand signals, and sometimes I move the boxes into little patterns even when people are telling me to stop.\n\nAt home it’s not really like that, I don’t think. It’s more tied to work. I know it sounds weird when I say it out loud, but I’m not trying to cause problems. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these patterns and behaviors are a part of who you are, or do they feel like something separate from yourself?\n\nPatient: I guess… it feels like me when I’m doing it, if that makes sense. Not like some totally separate thing taking over. It’s just… I get stuck in it at the warehouse, like with the forklift I’ll freeze with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, and the boxes start feeling like they should go in these little patterns.\n\nI know everyone’s yelling for me to stop sometimes, and I’m not trying to be difficult. It just sort of happens before I can explain it. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you want to do, or something that you feel like you shouldn't be doing?\n\nPatient: I don’t really want to be doing it if it’s causing trouble… I mean, I don’t go in thinking, “I’m going to mess things up today.” It just kind of happens at the warehouse.\n\nLike with the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’ll copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into these little patterns, and then people are yelling for me to stop. I know I probably shouldn’t be doing it then. I’m not trying to ignore them or be difficult… it just feels hard to explain in the moment. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really outside of work. Like, I don’t catch myself checking the door over and over or repeating stuff at home, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMaybe I double-check normal things sometimes, but I don’t think it’s a problem. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something you wish you could stop or control better?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do wish I could control it better. I don’t like that it makes everyone upset or slows things down.\n\nI mean, I know I’ve said it already, but at the warehouse I’ll just kind of freeze by the forklift with my arms up, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start putting boxes into these weird little patterns, and even when people are yelling at me to stop, I don’t always stop right away. I’m not trying to be a problem. I just… I wish I could catch it before it happens, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that are a part of you or something that you feel are foreign to yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… it feels like me in the sense that I’m the one doing it, so I don’t want to act like it’s someone else. But it doesn’t really feel like something I’d choose, if that makes sense.\n\nAt the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start moving boxes into those little patterns… it’s embarrassing, honestly. Especially when people are yelling for me to stop and I still don’t stop right away. That doesn’t feel like how I want to be. It feels kind of… not foreign exactly, but not like the normal me either. Is that an okay way to say it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to be doing, or something that you wish you could stop doing because they don't fit with how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t want to be doing it, no. I mean… I guess part of me tries to tell myself it’s not that bad, but it doesn’t really fit with how I see myself.\n\nLike at the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals, or moving boxes into weird little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop… that’s not something I’m proud of. I’m not doing it because I think it’s fun or because I want attention. I wish I could stop before it gets to that point.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are something that you would like to stop doing because they bother you or make you feel uncomfortable with yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I would like to stop. It does bother me, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it.\n\nLike, at work, when I end up standing there by the forklift with my arms up, or copying the beeping noises and the hand signals, or putting boxes into these odd little patterns… and people are yelling at me to stop, and I still don’t right away. That makes me feel pretty uncomfortable with myself after. I don’t want to be that guy at the warehouse causing trouble.\n\nI’m not trying to make excuses. I just… yeah, I wish I could stop doing it. Does that answer it right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are in line with how you want to behave?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I don’t want to be difficult or make it sound worse than it is, but it’s not how I want to act.\n\nAt the warehouse, when I freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, or move boxes into those strange little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop… that’s not really in line with me. I don’t want to be causing problems or looking like I’m doing it on purpose.\n\nI guess I wish I could catch myself sooner. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these behaviors are a part of who you want to be?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t want that to be who I am.\n\nI guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, or moving boxes into these strange little patterns even when everyone’s yelling for me to stop. And… that’s embarrassing to say, honestly.\n\nI don’t want people to see me like that. I want to be someone who just does his job and doesn’t cause problems. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any habits or behaviors that you feel compelled to do when you're not at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Um… not really that I can think of. I mean, nothing like the stuff at the warehouse.\n\nAt home I guess I do normal little things, like checking my phone or pacing a bit if I’m bored, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed anything that feels like I have to do it, at least not enough that I’d call it a problem.\n\nIs that okay? Like, if I remember something later I can tell you, but right now I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I mean, I don’t like that part. I try not to think of it as “me,” exactly, because that makes me feel worse.

It’s just… at the warehouse, when I freeze up with my arms raised by the forklift, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, or I put boxes into those odd little patterns even when people are telling me to stop… I know that looks bad. No… I don’t think I’d call it my normal self. I mean, I’m usually just trying to work and not cause trouble.

The warehouse stuff is what makes it feel not like me—freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeps and the hand signals, or putting boxes into those weird little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. I know that doesn’t sound normal.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think it is inconsistent with my values. I mean, I try to be respectful and do my work right, not make things harder for everybody.

And then at the warehouse, I guess they brought me in because I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and hand signals, or moving boxes into these strange little patterns even when people are yelling for me to stop. I know that’s not how I’m supposed to act there.

I don’t believe in ignoring people or causing problems at work, so… yes, it doesn’t really match what I think is right. I’m not trying to be difficult. Does that answer it okay?
